struct MoveCandidate {
    Move move;
    size_t next_move_count = 0;
    int distance_sq = 0; // squared distance from origin
};

int main() {
    srand(time(nullptr));

    // The command-line arguments provided by the user will determine the mode.
    string args = "greedy"; // replace with actual argument parsing
    if (args.empty() || args != "greedy") return 1;

    Board board;

    while(true) {
        vector<Move> current_moves = board.generate();
        if (current_moves.empty()) break;

        vector<MoveCandidate> candidates;
        candidates.reserve(current_moves.size());

        for (const Move& m : current_moves) {
            Board temp_board = board;
            temp_board.make_move(m);
            const auto& next_moves = temp_board.generate();
            size_t nc = next_moves.size();
            int distance = m.x * m.x + m.y * m.y;
            candidates.push_back({m, nc, distance});
        }

        // Compare by next_move_count (descending), then distance_sq (descending). 
        auto comp = [](const MoveCandidate& a, const MoveCandidate& b) {
            if (a.next_move_count != b.next_move_count) {
                return a.next_move_count > b.next_move_count;
            }
            return a.distance > b.distance;
        };

        const MoveCandidate& best_candidate = *max_element(candidates.begin(), candidates.end(), comp);
        Move selected_move = best_candidate.move;

#*************************************************************************
loglik.mix <- function(s,theta)
{
s\$z <- .na.to.snglcode(s\$z,999)
tp <- integer(s\$p); tq <- integer(s\$q)
ll <- .Fortran("lobsm",s\$q,s\$psi,s\$npsi,s\$ncells,theta\$sigma,
theta\$mu,theta\$pi,s\$npattz,s\$rz,tq,tq,s\$mdpzgrp,s\$npattw,s\$p,
s\$rw,tp,s\$mdpwgrp,s\$ngrp,s\$mobs,s\$mobsst,s\$nmobs,
s\$n,s\$z,s\$d,s\$jmp,integer(s\$p),ll=numeric(1),
PACKAGE="mix")\$ll
ll
}
#*************************************************************************
# Retrieves list of parameters from theta. If corr=F, returns a list
# containing an array of cell probabilities, a matrix of cell means, and
# a covariance matrix. If corr=T, returns a list containing an array of
# cell probabilities, a matrix of cell means, a vector of standard
# deviations, and a correlation matrix.
getparam.mix <- function(s,theta,corr=FALSE)
{
pii <- array(theta\$pi,s\$d)
if(!is.null(s\$wnames)){
pinames <- as.list(1:s\$p)
for(i in 1:s\$p){
pinames[[i]] <- paste(s\$wnames[i],"=",format(1:s\$d[i]),sep="")}
dimnames(pii) <- pinames}
mu <- theta\$mu*s\$sdv + s\$xbar
dimnames(mu) <- list(s\$znames,NULL)
sigma <- theta\$sigma[s\$psi]
sigma <- matrix(sigma,s\$q,s\$q)
tmp <- matrix(s\$sdv,s\$q,s\$q)
sigma <- sigma*tmp*t(tmp)
dimnames(sigma) <- list(s\$znames,s\$znames)
mu[,pii==0] <- NA
if(corr){
sdv <- sqrt(diag(sigma))
names(sdv) <- s\$znames
tmp <- matrix(sdv,s\$q,s\$q)
r <- sigma/(tmp*t(tmp)); dimnames(r) <- list(s\$znames,s\$znames)
result <- list(pi=pii,mu=mu,sdv=sdv,r=r)}
else result <- list(pi=pii,mu=mu,sigma=sigma)
result
}
#*************************************************************************
da.mix <- function(s,start,steps=1,prior=.5,showits=FALSE)
{
if(length(prior)==1) prior <- rep(prior,s\$ncells)
prior <- as.double(prior)
w <- !is.na(prior)
prior[!w] <- -999.0
s\$z <- .na.to.snglcode(s\$z,999)
s\$w <- .na.to.icode(s\$w,999)
tp <- integer(s\$p); tq <- integer(s\$q)
tmp <- .Fortran("tobsm",s\$q,s\$psi,s\$npsi,numeric(s\$npsi),s\$ncells,
matrix(0,s\$q,s\$ncells),numeric(s\$ncells),
s\$npattz,s\$rz,s\$mdpzgrp,s\$npattw,s\$p,s\$rw,s\$mdpwgrp,
s\$ngrp,s\$mobs,s\$mobsst,s\$nmobs,s\$n,s\$z,tp,tq,
PACKAGE="mix")
kn1 <- tmp[[4]];kn2 <- tmp[[6]];kn3 <- tmp[[7]]
sigma <- start\$sigma; pii <- start\$pi; mu <- start\$mu
t1 <- sigma; t2 <- mu; t3 <- pii
if(showits) cat(paste("Steps of Data Augmentation:","\n"))
for(i in 1:steps){
if(showits) cat(paste(format(i),"...",sep=""))
tmp <- .Fortran("istepm",s\$q,s\$psi,s\$npsi,s\$ncells,sigma,mu,pii,kn1,
kn2,kn3,t1=t1,t2=t2,t3=t3,s\$npattz,s\$rz,tq,tq,s\$mdpzgrp,
s\$npattw,s\$p,s\$rw,tp,s\$mdpwgrp,s\$ngrp,
s\$mobs,s\$mobsst,s\$nmobs,s\$n,s\$z,s\$d,s\$jmp,tp,kn3,kn1,s\$w,
numeric(s\$q), PACKAGE="mix")
t1 <- tmp\$t1; t2 <- tmp\$t2; t3 <- tmp\$t3
tmp <- .Fortran("pstepm",s\$q,s\$psi,s\$npsi,s\$ncells,sigma=t1,mu=t2,pi=t3,
s\$n,s\$p,as.double(prior),numeric(s\$npsi),matrix(0,s\$q,s\$q),
numeric(s\$q),tq,err=numeric(1), PACKAGE="mix")
{if(tmp\$err==1)
stop("Improper posterior--empty cells")
else{
sigma <- tmp\$sigma; mu <- tmp\$mu; pii <- tmp\$pi}}}
if(showits) cat("\n")
mu[,!w] <- NA
list(sigma=sigma,mu=mu,pi=pii)
}

imp.mix <- function(s,theta,x)
{
sigma <- theta\$sigma
mu <- theta\$mu
pii <- theta\$pi
z <- .na.to.snglcode(s\$z,999)
w <- .na.to.icode(s\$w,999)
tp <- integer(s\$p); tq <- integer(s\$q)
tmp <- .Fortran("istepm",s\$q,s\$psi,s\$npsi,s\$ncells,sigma,mu,pii,
sigma,mu,pii,sigma,mu,pii,s\$npattz,s\$rz,tq,tq,s\$mdpzgrp,
s\$np<#sep#>-

#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

vector<vector<int>> adj;
vector<int> leaves;
vector<bool> removed;
vector<int> parent;

pair<int, int> bfs(int start, int n) {
    vector<int> dist(n+1, -1);
    queue<int> q;
    q.push(start);
    dist[start] = 0;
    while (!q.empty()) {
        int u = q.front();
        q.pop();
        for (int v : adj[u]) {
            if (!removed[v] && dist[v] == -1 && v != parent[u]) {
                dist[v] = dist[u] + 1;
                parent[v] = u;
                q.push(v);
            }
        }
    }
    int max_dist = -1, node = start;
    for (int i = 1; i <= n; ++i) {
        if (!removed[i] && dist[i] > max_dist) {
            max_dist = dist[i];
            node = i;
        }
    }
    return {node, max_dist};
}

pair<int, int> get_diam(int n) {
    parent.assign(n+1, -1);
    int u = bfs(1, n).first;
    parent.assign(n+1, -1);
    auto [v, d] = bfs(u, n);
    return {u, v};
}

bool is_leaf(int u) {
    if (removed[u]) return false;
    int cnt = 0;
    for (int v : adj[u]) {
        if (!removed[v] && v != parent[u]) ++cnt;
    }
    return cnt == 0;
}

void find_leaves(int n, vector<int>& curr_leaves) {
    curr_leaves.clear();
    for (int i = 1; i <= n; ++i) {
        if (!removed[i] && is_leaf(i)) {
            curr_leaves.push_back(i);
        }
    }
}

int main() {
    ios::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(0);
    int n;
    cin >> n;
    adj.resize(n+1);
    for (int i = 0; i < n-1; ++i) {
        int u, v;
        cin >> u >> v;
        adj[u].push_back(v);
        adj[v].push_back(u);
    }
    removed.assign(n+1, false);
    vector<tuple<int, int, int>> operations;
    int total = 0;
    vector<int> curr_leaves;

    for (int step = 0; step < n-1; ++step) {
        auto [u, v] = get_diam(n);
        find_leaves(n, curr_leaves);
        int a = -1, b = -1;
        int max_dist = -1;
        for (int x : curr_leaves) {
            for (int y : curr_leaves) {
                if (x >= y) continue;
                parent.assign(n+1, -1);
                bfs(x, n);
                vector<int> path;
                int tmp = y;
                while (tmp != -1) {
                    path.push_back(tmp);
                    tmp = parent[tmp];
                }
                int dist = path.size() - 1;
                if (dist > max_dist) {
                    max_dist = dist;
                    a = x;
                    b = y;
                }
            }
        }
        if (a == -1) {
            a = curr_leaves[0];
            b = curr_leaves[1];
        }
        total += max_dist;
        int remove_node = b;
        removed[remove_node] = true;
        operations.emplace_back(a, b, remove_node);
    }

    cout << total << '\n';
    for (auto [a, b, c] : operations) {
        cout << a << ' ' << b << ' ' << c << '\n';
    }

    return 0;

So for example, if the sequence were written as "53, 51, 44, 32, ...", that's the string we create, and each of those characters has an ASCII code equal to the next term.

#*************************************************************************
ecm.mix <- function(s,margins,design,start,prior=1,maxits=1000,
showits=TRUE, eps=.0001)
{
if(length(prior)==1) prior <- rep(prior,s\$ncells)
prior <- as.double(prior)
w <- !is.na(prior)
prior[!w] <- -999.0
storage.mode(design) <- "double"; storage.mode(margins) <- "integer"
s\$z <- .na.to.snglcode(s\$z,999)
tp <- integer(s\$p); tq <- integer(s\$q)
sigma <- numeric(s\$npsi); pii <- numeric(s\$ncells)
mu <- matrix(0,s\$q,s\$ncells)
tmp <- .Fortran("tobsm",s\$q,s\$psi,s\$npsi,sigma,s\$ncells,mu,pii,
s\$npattz,s\$rz,s\$mdpzgrp,s\$npattw,s\$p,s\$rw,s\$mdpwgrp,
s\$ngrp,s\$mobs,s\$mobsst,s\$nmobs,s\$n,s\$z,tp,tq,
PACKAGE="mix")
kn1 <- tmp[[4]];kn2 <- tmp[[6]];kn3 <- tmp[[7]]
if(missing(start)){
tmp <- .Fortran("stvlm",s\$q,s\$psi,s\$npsi,sigma,s\$ncells,mu,
PACKAGE="mix")
sigma <- tmp[[4]];mu <- tmp[[6]]
pii <- rep(1,s\$ncells)
pii[!w] <- 0}
if(!missing(start)){
sigma <- start\$sigma; mu <- start\$mu; pii <- start\$pi
if(any(pii[w]==0)){
warning("Starting value on the boundary")}
if(any(!w)){
if(any(pii[!w]!=0)){
stop("Starting value has nonzero elements for structural zeros")}}}
eps1 <- .0000001*s\$n/sum(w)
r <- ncol(design); npsir <- as.integer(r*(r+1)/2); wk <- numeric(npsir)
tr <- integer(r); wkr <- numeric(r); wkd <- numeric(s\$ncells)
beta <- matrix(0,r,s\$q)
psir <- .Fortran("mkpsi",as.integer(r-1),matrix(as.integer(0),r,r),
PACKAGE="mix")[[2]]
converged <- FALSE; it <- 0
if(showits) cat(paste("Steps of ECM:","\n"))
t1 <- sigma; t2 <- mu; t3 <- pii
while((!converged) && (it < maxits) )
{
it <- it+1
if(showits) cat(paste(format(it),"...",sep=""))
if(it>1){sigma <- t1; mu <- t2; pii <- t3}
tmp <- .Fortran("estepm",s\$q,s\$psi,s\$npsi,s\$ncells,sigma,mu,pii,kn1,
kn2,kn3,t1,t2,t3,s\$npattz,s\$rz,tq,tq,s\$mdpzgrp,s\$npattw,
s\$p,s\$rw,tp,s\$mdpwgrp,s\$ngrp,
s\$mobs,s\$mobsst,s\$nmobs,s\$n,s\$z,s\$d,s\$jmp,tp,kn3,
PACKAGE="mix")
t1 <- tmp[[11]];t2 <- tmp[[12]];t3 <- tmp[[13]]
tmp <- .Fortran("mstepcm",s\$q,s\$psi,s\$npsi,s\$ncells,t1,t2,t3,sigma=t1,
mu=t2,s\$n,r,design,wk,tr,psir,npsir,wkr,wkd,beta,
PACKAGE="mix")
t1 <- tmp\$sigma; t2 <- tmp\$mu
t3[w] <- t3[w]+prior[w]-1
if(any(t3[w]<0))
stop("Estimate outside the parameter space. Check prior.")
t3 <- .Fortran("ipf",s\$ncells,t3,pii,length(margins),margins,
s\$p,s\$d,s\$jmp,tp,tp,tp,eps1, PACKAGE="mix")[[3]]
t3[w] <- t3[w]/sum(t3[w])
c3 <- all(abs(t3-pii)<=(eps*abs(pii)))
c2 <- all(abs(t2-mu)<=(eps*abs(mu)))
c1 <- all(abs(t1-sigma)<=(eps*abs(sigma)))
converged <- c3&c2&c1}
if(showits) cat("\n")
list(sigma=t1,mu=t2,pi=t3)
}

#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

struct Fraction {
    long long num, den;
    Fraction(long long n = 0, long long d = 1) : num(n), den(d) {
        reduce();
    }
    void reduce() {
        long long g = gcd(num, den);
        num /= g;
        den /= g;
        if (den < 0) {
            num *= -1;
            den *= -1;
        }
    }
    Fraction operator+(const Fraction& other) const {
        long long l = lcm(den, other.den);
        long long n = num * (l / den) + other.num * (l / other.den);
        return Fraction(n, l);
    }
    Fraction operator-() const {
        return Fraction(-num, den);
    }
    Fraction operator-(const Fraction& other) const {
        return *this + (-other);
    }
    Fraction operator*(const Fraction& other) const {
        return Fraction(num * other.num, den * other.den);
    }
    Fraction operator/(const Fraction& other) const {
        return Fraction(num * other.den, den * other.num);
    }
    bool is_zero() const {
        return num == 0;
    }
    bool is_integer() const {
        return den == 1;
    }
    long long to_int() const {
        return num / den;
    }
    static long long gcd(long long a, long long b) {
        while (b) {
            a %= b;
            swap(a, b);
        }
        return a;
    }
    static long long lcm(long long a, long long b) {
        return a / gcd(a, b) * b;
    }
};

vector<vector<Fraction>> invert_matrix(const vector<vector<long long>>& mat) {
    int n = mat.size();
    vector<vector<Fraction>> a(n, vector<Fraction>(2 * n));
    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        for (int j = 0; j < n; ++j) {
            a[i][j] = Fraction(mat[i][j], 1);
        }
        a[i][n + i] = Fraction(1, 1);
    }
    for (int col = 0; col < n; ++col) {
        int pivot = col;
        while (pivot < n && a[pivot][col].is_zero()) ++pivot;
        if (pivot == n) {
            assert(false);
        }
        swap(a[col], a[pivot]);
        Fraction inv = Fraction(1) / a[col][col];
        for (int j = col; j < 2 * n; ++j) {
            a[col][j] = a[col][j] * inv;
        }
        for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
            if (i != col && !a[i][col].is_zero()) {
                Fraction factor = a[i][col];
                for (int j = col; j < 2 * n; ++j) {
                    a[i][j] = a[i][j] - factor * a[col][j];
                }
            }
        }
    }
    vector<vector<Fraction>> inv(n, vector<Fraction>(n));
    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        for (int j = 0; j < n; ++j) {
            inv[i][j] = a[i][n + j];
        }
    }
    return inv;
}

vector<Fraction> multiply_matrix_vector(const vector<vector<Fraction>>& mat, const vector<Fraction>& vec) {
    int n = mat.size();
    vector<Fraction> res(n, Fraction(0, 1));
    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        for (int j = 0; j < n; ++j) {
            res[i] = res[i] + mat[i][j] * vec[j];
        }
    }
    return res;
}

vector<vector<long long>> generate_cyclic_shifts(const vector<long long>& b) {
    int n = b.size();
    vector<vector<long long>> mat(n, vector<long long>(n));
    for (int k = 0; k < n; ++k) {
        for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
            mat[k][i] = b[(i + k) % n];
        }
    }
    return mat;
}

int main() {
    ios_base::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(nullptr);
    
    int n;
    cin >> n;
    vector<long long> b(n), c(n);
    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        cin >> b[i];
    }
    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        cin >> c[i];
    }
    
    long long S_b = 0;
    for (long long x : b) {
        S_b += x * x;
    }
    
    vector<vector<long long>> M = generate_cyclic_shifts(b);
    vector<vector<Fraction>> M_frac(n, vector<Fraction>(n));
    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        for (int j = 0; j < n; ++j) {
            M_frac[i][j] = Fraction(M[i][j], 1);
        }
    }
    vector<vector<Fraction>> invM = invert_matrix(M);
    
    vector<Fraction> u(n);
    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        u[i] = Fraction(1, 2);
    }
    vector<Fraction> invM_u = multiply_matrix_vector(invM, u);
    
    vector<Fraction> w(n);
    for (int k = 0; k < n; ++k) {
        w[k] = Fraction(S_b - c[k], 2);
    }
    vector<Fraction> invM_w = multiply_matrix_vector(invM, w);
    
    Fraction A(0, 1);
    Fraction B(0, 1);
    Fraction C(0, 1);
    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        A = A + invM_u[i] * invM_u[i];
        B = B + invM_u[i] * invM_w[i];
        C = C + invM_w[i] * invM_w[i];
    }
    B = B * Fraction(2, 1);
    B = B - Fraction(1, 1);
    
    long long lcm_den = 1;
    lcm_den = Fraction::lcm(lcm_den, A.den);
    lcm_den = Fraction::lcm(lcm_den, B.den);
    lcm_den = Fraction::lcm(lcm_den, C.den);
    
    long long A_num = A.num * (lcm_den / A.den);
    long long B_num = B.num * (lcm_den / B.den);
    long long C_num = C.num * (lcm_den / C.den);
    
    long long discriminant = B_num * B_num - 4 * A_num * C_num;
    if (discriminant < 0) {
        cout << "0\n";
        return 0;
    }
    long long sqrt_disc = sqrt(discriminant);
    if (sqrt_disc * sqrt_disc != discriminant) {
        cout << "0\n";
        return 0;
    }
    
    vector<long long> candidates;
    for (int sign : {1, -1}) {
        long long numerator = -B_num + sign * sqrt_disc;
        long long denominator = 2 * A_num;
        if (denominator == 0) continue;
        if (numerator % denominator != 0) continue;
        long long Sa = numerator / denominator;
        if (Sa >= 0) {
            candidates.push_back(Sa);
        }
    }
    
    vector<vector<long long>> valid_a;
    for (long long Sa : candidates) {
        bool valid = true;
        for (int k = 0; k < n; ++k) {
            long long tmp = Sa + S_b - c[k];
            if (tmp % 2 != 0) {
                valid = false;
                break;
            }
        }
        if (!valid) continue;
        
        vector<long long> a(n);
        bool all_integer = true;
        for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
            Fraction ai = invM_u[i] * Fraction(Sa, 1) + invM_w[i];
            if (!ai.is_integer()) {
                all_integer = false;
                break;
            }
            a[i] = ai.to_int();
        }
        if (!all_integer) continue;
        
        long long sum_sq = 0;
        for (long long x : a) {

Then, the linear terms: ky + kdx + kx + kdy = k(y + dx + x + dy) = k[ (d + 1)x + (1 + d)y ]

Plus 2d.

So LHS is k²(x + y)^2 + k(d + 1)(x + y) + 2d

Now, RHS:

f(x + f(y))·f(y + f(x)) + f(f(x)) + y

First compute f(y) = ky + d and f(x) = kx + d.

So, x + f(y) = x + ky + d

Similarly, y + f(x) = y + kx + d

Then, f(x + f(y)) = k(x + ky + d) + d = kx + k²y + kd + d

Similarly, f(y + f(x)) = k(y + kx + d) + d = ky + k²x + kd + d

Multiply them:

[kx + k²y + kd + d][ky + k²x + kd + d]

This looks complicated. Let's compute that product term.

Let me denote A = kx + k²y + kd + d and B = ky + k²x + kd + d. Then A*B:

(kx + k²y + kd + d)(ky + k²x + kd + d)

Twelfth term: kd * d = k d²

Thirteenth term: d * ky = k d y

Sixteenth term: d * d = d²

if(showits) cat("\n")
list(sigma=sigma,mu=mu,pi=pii)
}
#*************************************************************************
# multiple imputation inference
mi.inference <- function(est,std.err,confidence=.95)
{
qstar <- est[[1]]
for(i in 2:length(est)){qstar <- cbind(qstar,est[[i]])}
qbar <- apply(qstar,1,mean)
u <- std.err[[1]]
for(i in 2:length(std.err)){u <- cbind(u,std.err[[i]])}
dimnames(u)[[1]] <- dimnames(qstar)[[1]]
u <- u^2
ubar <- apply(u,1,mean)
bm <- apply(qstar,1,var)
m <- dim(qstar)[2]
tm <- ubar+((1+(1/m))*bm)
rem <- (1+(1/m))*bm/ubar
nu <- (m-1)*(1+(1/rem))**2
alpha <- 1-(1-confidence)/2
low <- qbar-qt(alpha,nu)*sqrt(tm)
up <- qbar+qt(alpha,nu)*sqrt(tm)
pval <- 2*(1-pt(abs(qbar/sqrt(tm)),nu))
fminf <- (rem+2/(nu+3))/(rem+1)
result <- list(est=qbar,std.err=sqrt(tm),df=nu,signif=pval,lower=low,
upper=up,r=rem,fminf=fminf)
result
}

        if (!valid) continue;
        
        vector<long long> a(n);

            Fraction ai = invM_u[i] * Fraction(Sa, 1) + invM_w[i];
            if (!ai.is_integer()) {
                all_integer = false;
                break;
            }
            a[i] = ai.to_int();
        }

        }
        if (sum_sq != Sa) continue;

}<#sep#> k² = 0 ⇒ k²(k - 1) = 0 ⇒ k = 0 or k = 1.

Similarly for y² terms:

Left: k²

Right: k³

Same equation, so same solution k=0 or k=1.

Now, let's check k=0 and k=1.

Case 1: k=0.

If k=0, then f(x) = d (constant function). But we already checked constant functions and saw they don't work unless d satisfies 2d = d² + d + y for all y, which is impossible. So k=0 is invalid.

Case 2: k=1.

Then, k=1, so let's substitute k=1 into the equations.

For x² terms: Left had 1, Right had 1³=1. So okay.

For y² terms: Similarly.

Now, xy terms:

Left: 2k²xy = 2xy

Right: (k² + k^4)xy. With k=1, that's (1 + 1)xy = 2xy. So that's equal. Good.

Next, x terms:

Left: k(d + 1)x = (d + 1)x

Right: (k³ + 2k² + k)d x + k²x. Since k=1:

(1 + 2 + 1)d x + 1²x = (4d +1)x

So equate (d + 1)x = (4d + 1)x ⇒ d + 1 = 4d + 1 ⇒ 0 = 3d ⇒ d=0.

Similarly for y terms:

Left: (d + 1)y

Right: (k³ + 2k² + k)d y + y. With k=1:

(1 + 2 +1)d y + y = 4d y + y

Set equal to left: (d + 1)y = 4d y + y ⇒ (d +1) = 4d +1 ⇒ 0 = 3d ⇒ d=0.

Constant terms:

Left: 2d

Right: d²(k +1)^2 + kd + d. With k=1 and d=0:

d²(2)^2 + 1*0 + 0 = 0 + 0 +0. So right side is 0. Left side is 2*0=0. So that's okay.

Thus, with k=1 and d=0, we have f(x) = x. So that works. Which matches our earlier test.

So f(x) = x is a solution. But is it the only solution?

We need to check if there are other functions that might satisfy the equation. Let me see if we can find another approach.

Let me try setting y = 0 again. Wait, when I set y=0 earlier, the equation became:

f(x·f(x)) + f(x) = f(f(x))·[f(x + f(0)) + 1]

If f(x) = x, then f(0) = 0. So plugging in, we have:

f(x·x) + f(x) = f(f(x))·[f(x + 0) + 1] → f(x²) + x = x·(x +1) → x² + x = x² +x, which holds.

But if f is not linear, perhaps we can deduce something else.

Alternatively, let's assume that f is injective or surjective. Let's check if f is injective.

Suppose f(a) = f(b). Let's see if we can show a = b. Maybe by setting x and y such that we can get an equation involving a and b.

Alternatively, perhaps try to find f(0). Let me set x=0 and y=0.

Set x=0, y=0:

LHS: f(0 + 0·f(0)) + f(0 + 0·f(0)) = f(0) + f(0) = 2f(0)

RHS: f(0 + f(0))·f(0 + f(0)) + f(f(0)) + 0 = [f(f(0))]^2 + f(f(0))

So equation is 2f(0) = [f(f(0))]^2 + f(f(0))

Let’s denote c = f(0). Then the equation becomes 2c = [f(c)]² + f(c)

But we need more information. Let's see if we can relate c to f(c).

If f(x) = x, then c=0, so 0 = [0]^2 + 0, which holds. If there's another solution, we need another c.

Alternatively, let's assume that f is invertible. Maybe that helps.

Alternatively, try to find f(f(x)). Let me consider substituting y such that x + y = 0. Let’s set y = -x. Then the original equation becomes:

f(-x + x·f(0)) + f(x + (-x)·f(0)) = f(x + f(-x))·f(-x + f(x)) + f(f(x)) + (-x)

Simplify:

First term inside f: -x + x·f(0) = x(f(0) -1)

Second term: x -x·f(0) = x(1 - f(0))

So LHS: f(x(f(0) -1)) + f(x(1 - f(0)))

RHS: f(x + f(-x))·f(-x + f(x)) + f(f(x)) - x

This might be too general. Let me check with f(x) = x.

If f(x)=x, then f(0)=0. So:

LHS: f(x(-1)) + f(x(1)) = f(-x) + f(x) = -x + x = 0

RHS: f(x + (-x))·f(-x + x) + f(f(x)) -x = f(0)·f(0) + f(x) -x = 0·0 +x -x = 0, which matches.

But for a general f, this might not hold. Not sure if that helps.

Another approach: Let's suppose that f is additive, meaning f(a + b) = f(a) + f(b). But additive functions over R without continuity can be pathological, but if we assume continuity (which we can't here, since it's not given), then f(x) = kx. But since we already have f(x) = x as a solution, maybe that's the only additive solution.

Alternatively, maybe try to see if f is its own inverse. Wait, if f(f(x)) = x, then perhaps. If f is an involution, but I don't know if that helps.

Wait, let's try to see if the function is injective. Suppose f(a) = f(b). Let's try to find a relation.

Let’s suppose f(a) = f(b). Let me set x = a and x = b in some equation. Maybe when we set y = something. Alternatively, maybe plugging into the original equation.

Wait, take y = something. For example, set x = a, and set y = t - a·f(a + t), but that might not help.

f(a·f(a)) + f(a) = f(f(a))·[f(a + f(0)) + 1]

If f(a) = f(b), then:

For x=a: f(a·f(a)) + f(a) = f(f(a))·[f(a + f(0)) + 1]

Similarly, for x=b: f(b·f(b)) + f(b) = f(f(b))·[f(b + f(0)) + 1]

But since f(a) = f(b), let’s denote c = f(a) = f(b). Then:

For x=b: f(b·c) + c = f(c)·[f(b + f(0)) + 1]

The process is that every term in the sequence is the ASCII code of the corresponding character in the printed string. 

The challenge is to find a sequence, such that when printed with commas and spaces between elements (like a typical OEIS listing), each of those characters has an ASCII code equal to their sequence element. 

The first terms given in the problem are correct. 

Now, how do we construct such a sequence?

Since it's listed as the unique sequence with this property, the approach is likely to model it as a fixed point in the conversion process. 

That is, suppose we have a sequence S, and we can convert S into a string T which is the standard representation (numbers separated by ', '), then convert T into an ASCII sequence, which we call A (A has length equal to the length of T), then we need A equals S (the original sequence). 

To compute this, the sequence can be generated by iterating this process until it converges, starting from an initial guess, but since it's unique, this process would eventually settle into the correct sequence. 

Alternatively, perhaps we can model it as an infinite loop, but given that the initial terms are given, maybe we can write a program that follows the observation that each term is determined by the needed ASCII codes and the necessary characters in the constructed string. 

Alternatively, for programming purposes, perhaps it can be represented via a loop where the sequence is built incrementally by ensuring that each new term matches the required ASCII code for the next character. 

Wait, but how does this loop work? Because the sequence has to be self-referential. 

Let me think of it this way:

The sequence is S = [s₁, s₂, s₃, ...]

Then the string representation T must be constructed as "s₁, s₂, s₃, ...".

Each character in T has code equal to s_i where s_i is the code. 

So the first term in the sequence is s₁= (the ASCII of the first character of T's string). 

The second term is s₂= ASCII of the second character, and so on. 

So the issue is that the string T must be exactly the same as the sequence's own terms, with separation via commas and spaces, and each character in that string must translate to the sequence's numbers. 

This seems like a chicken and egg problem, so the approach might need to be creative. 

Perhaps the key insight is that the sequence must include its own ASCII codes. The sequence has to, in its string form, when each character is taken's ASCII code, produce itself. 

So, to code this, we can model the process as building the string step-by-step, representing each pending part of the sequence as a target for the next code. 

Alternatively, think of the sequence as being built by a program that generated the sequence's code in such a way that the self-referential loop is maintained. But in programming terms, we can't encode it in a simple iterative way because building the string requires knowing what the terms are.

The main focus of Intermediate Algebra, 5th edition, is to address the fundamental needs of today's developmental math students. Offering a uniquely modern, balanced program, Intermediate Algebra, 5th edition, integrates conceptual understanding with traditional skill and practice reinforced through visual and interactive practice in Enhanced WebAssign, available exclusively from Cengage Learning. By helping students understand the language of algebra and the "why" behind problem solving through instructional approaches and worked examples, they are better equipped to succeed at the "how." Practice is essential in making these connections and it is emphasized in Intermediate Algebra, 5th edition, with additional practice problems both in the text and Enhanced WebAssign. Give your students confidence by showing them how Algebra is not just about the x…it's also about the why.

#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

const int MOD = 1e9 + 7;

string subtract_one(string s) {
    int n = s.size();
    int i = n - 1;
    while (i >= 0 && s[i] == '0') {
        s[i] = '9';
        i--;
    }
    if (i == -1) return "0";
    s[i]--;
    if (s[i] == '0' && i == 0 && n > 1) {
        return s.substr(1);
    }
    return s;
}

int count_valid(const string &s, int m, int d) {
    int n = s.size();
    vector<int> digits(n);
    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        digits[i] = s[i] - '0';
    }
    int res = 0;
    vector<vector<vector<int>>> dp(n+1, vector<vector<int>>(m, vector<int>(2, 0)));
    dp[0][0][1] = 1;
    for (int pos = 0; pos < n; ++pos) {
        for (int mod = 0; mod < m; ++mod) {
            for (int tight : {0, 1}) {
                if (dp[pos][mod][tight] == 0) continue;
                int current_pos = pos + 1;
                if (current_pos % 2 == 0) {
                    int digit = d;
                    int new_tight = 0;
                    if (tight) {
                        if (digit > digits[pos]) continue;
                        new_tight = (digit == digits[pos]);
                    }
                    int new_mod = (mod * 10 + digit) % m;
                    if (current_pos == n) {
                        if (new_mod == 0) {
                            res = (res + dp[pos][mod][tight]) % MOD;
                        }
                    } else {
                        dp[pos+1][new_mod][new_tight] = (dp[pos+1][new_mod][new_tight] + dp[pos][mod][tight]) % MOD;
                    }
                } else {
                    int start = (current_pos == 1 && n > 1) ? 1 : 0;
                    for (int digit = 0; digit <= 9; ++digit) {
                        if (digit == d) continue;
                        if (digit < start) continue;
                        if (tight) {
                            if (digit > digits[pos]) continue;
                            bool new_tight_flag = (digit == digits[pos]);
                            int new_mod = (mod * 10 + digit) % m;
                            if (current_pos == n) {
                                if (new_mod == 0) {
                                    res = (res + dp[pos][mod][tight]) % MOD;
                                }
                            } else {
                                dp[pos+1][new_mod][new_tight_flag] = (dp[pos+1][new_mod][new_tight_flag] + dp[pos][mod][tight]) % MOD;
                            }
                        } else {
                            int new_mod = (mod * 10 + digit) % m;
                            if (current_pos == n) {
                                if (new_mod == 0) {
                                    res = (res + dp[pos][mod][tight]) % MOD;
                                }
                            } else {
                                dp[pos+1][new_mod][0] = (dp[pos+1][new_mod][0] + dp[pos][mod][tight]) % MOD;
                            }
                        }
                    }
                }
            }
        }
    }
    return res;
}

int main() {
    ios_base::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(nullptr);
    int m, d;
    string a, b;
    cin >> m >> d >> a >> b;
    string lower = subtract_one(a);
    int n = a.size();
    int cnt_b = count_valid(b, m, d);
    int cnt_lower = 0;
    if (lower.size() == n) {
        if (lower[0] != '0' || n == 1) {
            cnt_lower = count_valid(lower, m, d);
        }
    }
    int ans = (cnt_b - cnt_lower + MOD) % MOD;
    cout << ans << '\n';
    return 0;

If f is injective, then from f(a·c) = f(b·c) ⇒ a·c = b·c. If c ≠ 0, then a = b. If c = 0, then f(a) = 0, and we need to check if this implies a is unique. But we don't know if f is injective yet. So perhaps this line of thought isn't helpful.

Let me think differently. Suppose we set x + y = 0, so y = -x.

Then original equation becomes:

f(-x + x·f(0)) + f(x + (-x)·f(0)) = f(x + f(-x))·f(-x + f(x)) + f(f(x)) - x

But this seems complicated.

Alternatively, let's try to assume that f is linear, which we already saw that f(x) = x works. Maybe that's the only solution. But how do we know?

Alternatively, suppose that there exists some a such that f(a) = 0. Let me see if that's possible.

Suppose there exists a with f(a) = 0. Then let's set x = a and y such that x + y = something. Maybe set y = a - x. Wait, not sure. Alternatively, set y = a.

Set x = a and y = a:

Original equation:

f(a + a·f(2a)) + f(a + a·f(2a)) = f(a + f(a))·f(a + f(a)) + f(f(a)) + a

Since f(a) =0, this becomes:

f(a + a·f(2a)) + f(a + a·f(2a)) = f(a + 0)·f(a + 0) + f(0) + a

So 2f(a + a·f(2a)) = f(a)^2 + f(0) + a

But f(a) =0, so:

2f(a + a·f(2a)) = 0 + f(0) + a

But without knowing f(2a) and f(0), this is difficult.

Alternatively, let's try to set x = y. Let me try that.

Set x = y. Then the equation becomes:

f(x + x·f(2x)) + f(x + x·f(2x)) = f(x + f(x))·f(x + f(x)) + f(f(x)) + x

So:

2f(x + x·f(2x)) = [f(x + f(x))]^2 + f(f(x)) + x

If f(x) = x, then x + x·f(2x) = x + x·2x = x + 2x². Then f(x +2x²) = x +2x². Then left side is 2(x + 2x²). Right side: [f( x +x )]^2 + f(x) +x = [f(2x)]^2 +x +x = (2x)^2 +2x =4x² +2x. So 2(x +2x²) =4x² +2x. Which is 2x +4x² =4x² +2x. Correct. So it holds.

But again, not helpful for finding other solutions.

Another strategy: Let's suppose that f is surjective. Then for any z ∈ R, there exists some a ∈ R such that f(a) = z. Let me see if I can use that.

Alternatively, let's see if we can find f(f(x)). Maybe set y = something to get f(f(x)).

Wait, when we set x=0 earlier, we had:

f(y) + f(y·f(y)) = f(f(y))·f(y + f(0)) + f(f(0)) + y

If we can solve for f(f(y)), maybe we can find an expression.

But this seems complicated. Let's try specific values.

Suppose we set y =1. Then:

f(1) + f(f(1)) = f(f(1))·f(1 + f(0)) + f(f(0)) +1

But unless we know f(0), it's difficult.

Wait, perhaps from the earlier case when we set x=0 and y=0, we had 2f(0) = [f(f(0))]^2 + f(f(0))

Let’s denote c = f(0). Then 2c = [f(c)]² + f(c). Let's call d = f(c). So 2c = d² + d.

Also, from setting y=0 and x=0, but maybe other substitutions can relate c and d.

Alternatively, perhaps if we set x = -f(0) in the equation where y=0.

Wait, when y=0, we have:

f(x·f(x)) + f(x) = f(f(x))·[f(x + f(0)) +1]

If we set x = -f(0), then:

f(-f(0)·f(-f(0))) + f(-f(0)) = f(f(-f(0)))·[f(-f(0) + f(0)) +1] = f(f(-f(0)))·[f(0) +1]

But this seems complicated.

Alternatively, suppose that f is invertible. Then maybe we can substitute y = f^{-1}(z) for some z. But without knowing if f is invertible, it's hard to proceed.

Alternatively, let's look back at the original equation and see if swapping x and y gives any symmetry. The original equation is:

f(y + x f(x + y)) + f(x + y f(x + y)) = f(x + f(y)) f(y + f(x)) + f(f(x)) + y

If we swap x and y:

f(x + y f(y + x)) + f(y + x f(y + x)) = f(y + f(x)) f(x + f(y)) + f(f(y)) + x

But since addition is commutative, x + y = y + x, so the left side becomes the same as original left side. The right side swaps f(x + f(y)) and f(y + f(x)) and adds x instead of y. So:

Original RHS: f(x + f(y)) f(y + f(x)) + f(f(x)) + y

Swapped RHS: f(y + f(x)) f(x + f(y)) + f(f(y)) + x

Which is the same as the original RHS except for the last term and the f(f(x)) term.

Thus, comparing original equation and swapped equation:

Original: LHS = RHS_orig = f(x + f(y)) f(y + f(x)) + f(f(x)) + y

Swapped: LHS = RHS_swapped = f(x + f(y)) f(y + f(x)) + f(f(y)) + x

Therefore, since LHS is symmetric in x and y, RHS_orig and RHS_swapped must be equal when swapping x and y. Hence:

f(x + f(y)) f(y + f(x)) + f(f(x)) + y = f(y + f(x)) f(x + f(y)) + f(f(y)) + x

But the first term is the same on both sides. So subtract those:

f(f(x)) + y = f(f(y)) + x

So, for all x, y:

f(f(x)) + y = f(f(y)) + x

Let me rearrange this:

f(f(x)) - x = f(f(y)) - y

But this must hold for all x and y. The left side depends only on x, the right side only on y. Therefore, both sides must be equal to the same constant. Let's denote that constant as k. So:

f(f(x)) - x = k for all x

Similarly, f(f(y)) - y = k for all y. Hence,

f(f(x)) = x + k

Now, substituting back into the equation f(f(x)) = x +k, let's use this in the previous equation where swapping x and y:

From the original and swapped equations, we derived that f(f(x)) + y = f(f(y)) + x. Substitute f(f(x)) =x +k and f(f(y))= y +k:

(x + k) + y = (y + k) + x → x + k + y = y + k + x, which holds. So this is consistent.

Thus, we have that f(f(x)) =x +k for some constant k.

But we also had from setting x=0 and y=0: 2c = [f(c)]² + f(c), where c = f(0).

But with f(f(x))<#sep#> perhaps the key is to realize that the comma, space-separated list of the numbers in the sequence must produce a string whose ASCII values are exactly the terms. 

Therefore, the first number must be the ASCII code of the first character of the representation, which is the first digit of the first term. The second number is the second digit (or part of another digit). 

This is a bit recursive. Let me think of building the sequence digit by digit. 

Each character added to the string corresponds to the next number in the sequence, but when that character is part of a number in the sequence's string representation, it influences the next terms. 

So this requires that the way the numbers are written (with commas and spaces) must form a stream of characters that exactly gives their own ASCII codes. 

Assuming that the complete sequence is fixed, the algorithm can be to code it in a way that, for each position n in the sequence, the nth character in the string is the character with ASCII code S[n], and the string must contain exactly the numbers separated by ", " in order. 

This is quite a complex problem. 

Let me see how this might be achievable in code. For example, writing a code that outputs the sequence by interpreting the program's text as the source of the characters, taking their ASCII codes to form the numbers. However, the problem requires that the sequence is generated correctly regardless of the program used, so perhaps the best approach is to model it as a self-descriptive sequence. 

Alternatively, the sequence can be thought of as:

The ASCII codes of each character in the following string:

"53, 51, 44, 32, 53, 49, 44, 32, 52, 52, 44, 32, ..."

Which gives the sequence itself. Therefore, the code's goal is to recreate that pattern. 

But how can we generate this without hardcoding?

Perhaps a way to model the sequence incrementally. Let's suppose we start building the sequence by writing down the first character's code as the first term. But how do we know what the first character is?

Then terms 13-16: 51,50,44,32 → '5','0', etc. 

Hmm. 

Looking at the sequence in groups of 4:

Each group of four terms forms a number followed by the comma and space. The first element in the group is the first digit of the number, the second the second digit (if any), the third is comma, and fourth is space. Wait except that numbers may have varying digits. 

Looking at the first group (terms1-4 ->53,51,44,32): 

'5' and '3' make 53 the number, then comma and space.

Hmm! So this seems to suggest that the first two digits (of each group of four) give the next number. So each "number" in the sequence is presented with exactly two digits. 

So every number in the sequence is a two-digit number. Since their ASCII codes must form two-digit numbers. 

Wait, but 44 is two digits, comma is 44 which is a valid ASCII (the comma), so the numbers in the sequence can be two-digit. 

So each number in the sequence is two-digit, represented as two characters in the sequence string, followed by a comma and a space, which take one and one character respectively (so total four characters per number, except perhaps leading ones?). 

So for each two-digit number x in the sequence, it is represented by its two ASCII digits, then a comma and a space. 

Therefore, the coding is that the entire sequence is made up of two-digit numbers, separated by comma and space. 

Hence, the nth character in the string corresponds to the digit of the sequence's written text. 

This structure suggests that the sequence entries are two-digit numbers, so each number must be between 32 (space, minimum ASCII) and 255, but given the starting numbers, it's between 32 and 5X (53 etc. Up to 57 perhaps). 

The sequence is thus a list of two-digit numbers, each separated by ", ". 

Therefore, the entire sequence's text is a series of two-digit numbers separated by ", ", and the ASCII codes of each character in that string is exactly the list of those numbers (two digits followed by 44,32 etc). 

Therefore, the entire sequence is made by concatenating the two-number codes, along with their comma-space separators. 

So the problem reduces to generating a self-referential string where each two-digit number is part of the sequence, and their sequence of the numbers' digits plus comma and space characters form the ASCII codes of themselves. 

This gives us a way to possibly model the sequence generation step by step:

We can model the entire string as the concatenation of the elements' two-digit codes, separated by ", ".

Let me denote the current state as being a certain length, and we can generate the next character based on what comes next in the sequence. 

Alternatively, think of the sequence as a list where each entry is a two-digit number, so the text is built as "A,B,C,D,...", where A,B are two-digit etc. 

Then the codes for each character must equal the sequence list.

## Features

• Read It links under each question quickly jump to the corresponding section of a complete, interactive eBook that lets students highlight and take notes as they read. Videos are also integrated into the eBook Read It links for a just-in-time learning approach.
• A Personal Study Plan lets your students gauge their mastery of the material through chapter and section assessments and generates individualized study plans that include various online, interactive multimedia resources.
• Are You Ready (AYR) problems review crucial prerequisite skills students need to be successful with application questions.
• Master It Tutorials (CMI) show students how to solve a similar problem in multiple steps by providing direction along with derivation, so the student understands the concepts.

#include <iostream>
#include <string>
#include <algorithm>

using namespace std;

string multiply(string num, int x) {
    reverse(num.begin(), num.end());
    string res;
    int carry = 0;
    for (char c : num) {
        int digit = c - '0';
        int product = digit * x + carry;
        res.push_back(product % 10 + '0');
        carry = product / 10;
    }
    while (carry > 0) {
        res.push_back(carry % 10 + '0');
        carry /= 10;
    }
    reverse(res.begin(), res.end());
    return res;
}

string divide(string num, int x) {
    string res;
    int carry = 0;
    bool leading_zero = true;
    for (char c : num) {
        int digit = c - '0';
        int current = carry * 10 + digit;
        int q = current / x;
        carry = current % x;
        if (leading_zero && q == 0) {
            continue;
        }
        leading_zero = false;
        res.push_back(q + '0');
    }
    if (res.empty()) {
        res = "0";
    }
    return res;
}

int compare(const string& a, const string& b) {
    if (a.size() != b.size()) {
        return a.size() > b.size() ? 1 : -1;
    }
    for (int i = 0; i < a.size(); ++i) {
        if (a[i] != b[i]) {
            return a[i] > b[i] ? 1 : -1;
        }
    }
    return 0;
}

string comb(int a, int b) {
    if (b == 0 || b == a) {
        return "1";
    }
    if (b > a) {
        return "0";
    }
    string result = "1";
    for (int i = 1; i <= b; ++i) {
        int numerator = a - b + i;
        result = multiply(result, numerator);
        result = divide(result, i);
    }
    return result;
}

string subtract(string a, string b) {
    reverse(a.begin(), a.end());
    reverse(b.begin(), b.end());
    string res;
    int carry = 0;
    for (int i = 0; i < a.size(); ++i) {
        int digit_a = a[i] - '0';
        int digit_b = (i < b.size() ? b[i] - '0' : 0);
        int sub = digit_a - digit_b - carry;
        if (sub < 0) {
            sub += 10;
            carry = 1;
        } else {
            carry = 0;
        }
        res.push_back(sub + '0');
    }
    while (res.size() > 1 && res.back() == '0') {
        res.pop_back();
    }
    reverse(res.begin(), res.end());
    int pos = 0;
    while (pos < res.size() && res[pos] == '0') {
        pos++;
    }
    if (pos == res.size()) {
        return "0";
    }
    return res.substr(pos);
}

string build_string(int n, int k, string m) {
    string res;
    int remaining_ones = k;
    int remaining_length = n;
    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        if (remaining_ones == 0) {
            res.append(remaining_length, '0');
            break;
        }
        if (remaining_ones == remaining_length) {
            res.append(remaining_length, '1');
            break;
        }
        int a = remaining_length - 1;
        int b = remaining_ones;
        string c = comb(a, b);
        if (compare(c, m) >= 0) {
            res += '0';
        } else {
            res += '1';
            m = subtract(m, c);
            remaining_ones--;
        }
        remaining_length--;
    }
    return res;
}

int main() {
    int T;
    cin >> T;
    while (T--) {
        int N, K;
        string M;
        cin >> N >> K >> M;

        int pos = 0;
        while (pos < M.size() && M[pos] == '0') {
            pos++;
        }
        if (pos == M.size()) {
            M = "0";
        } else {
            M = M.substr(pos);
        }

        string total = comb(N, K);
        if (compare(total, M) < 0) {
            cout << -1 << endl;
        } else {
            string s = build_string(N, K, M);
            cout << s << endl;
        }
    }
    return 0;

#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

typedef long long ll;

struct Line {
    mutable ll a, b;
    mutable long double x;

    Line(ll a, ll b) : a(a), b(b), x(0) {}

    bool operator<(const Line& other) const { return a < other.a; }
    bool operator<(ll q) const { return x < q; }
};

struct DynamicHull : multiset<Line, less<>> {
    static const ll INF = 1e18;

    ll div(ll a, ll b) {
        return a / b - ((a ^ b) < 0 && a % b);
    }

    bool isect(iterator x, iterator y) {
        if (y == end()) {
            x->x = INF;
            return false;
        }
        if (x->a == y->a) {
            x->x = (x->b > y->b) ? INF : -INF;
        } else {
            x->x = div(y->b - x->b, x->a - y->a);
        }
        return x->x >= y->x;
    }

    void add(ll a, ll b) {
        auto z = insert(Line(a, b)), y = z++, x = y;
        while (isect(y, z)) z = erase(z);
        if (x != begin() && isect(--x, y)) isect(x, y = erase(y));
        while ((y = x) != begin() && (--x)->x >= y->x) isect(x, erase(y));
    }

    void remove(ll a, ll b) {
        auto it = find(Line(a, b));
        if (it == end()) return;
        auto prev = it, next = it;
        if (it != begin()) --prev;
        ++next;

        erase(it);
        if (prev != end() && next != end()) {
            if (prev->a == next->a) {
                if (prev->b < next->b) erase(prev);
                else erase(next);
            } else {
                long double x = div(next->b - prev->b, prev->a - next->a);
                prev->x = x;
            }
        }
    }

    ll query(ll q) {
        if (empty()) return -INF;
        auto line = lower_bound(q);
        if (line != begin()) --line;
        return line->a * q + line->b;
    }
};

int main() {
    ios_base::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(nullptr);

    int n;
    cin >> n;

    vector<tuple<ll, ll, int>> added;
    DynamicHull hull;
    vector<bool> active;

    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        int t;
        cin >> t;
        if (t == 1) {
            ll a, b;
            cin >> a >> b;
            added.emplace_back(a, b, i);
            active.push_back(true);
            hull.add(a, b);
        } else if (t == 2) {
            int idx;
            cin >> idx;
            --idx;
            auto [a, b, pos] = added[idx];
            if (active[idx]) {
                hull.remove(a, b);
                active[idx] = false;
            }
        } else {
            ll q;
            cin >> q;
            ll res = hull.query(q);
            if (res == -DynamicHull::INF) {
                cout << "EMPTY SET\n";
            } else {
                cout << res << '\n';
            }
        }
    }

    return 0;
}<#sep#>2. So f(z)=z +k/2.

Now, let's check if f(z)=z +k/2 satisfies the original equation:

Original equation:

f(y +x·f(x+y)) +f(x + y·f(x+y)) = f(x +f(y))·f(y +f(x)) +f(f(x)) +y

Substitute f(z)=z +k/2:

Left-hand side:

f(y +x·(x+y +k/2)) +f(x + y·(x+y +k/2))

Compute each argument:

First argument: y +x(x + y +k/2) = y +x² +xy + (xk)/2

Second argument: x +y(x + y +k/2)=x +xy + y² + (yk)/2

Apply f to each:

First term: [y +x² +xy + (xk)/2] +k/2 = y +x² +xy + (xk)/2 +k/2

Second term: [x +xy + y² + (yk)/2] +k/2 =x +xy + y² + (yk)/2 +k/2

Sum LHS:

(y +x² +xy + (xk)/2 +k/2) + (x +xy + y² + (yk)/2 +k/2)

= x² + y² + 2xy +x + y + (xk + yk)/2 +k

RHS:

f(x +f(y))·f(y +f(x)) +f(f(x)) +y

Compute each part:

f(y) =y +k/2, f(x)=x +k/2

So, x +f(y)=x +y +k/2

y +f(x)=y +x +k/2

Thus, f(x +f(y))= (x +y +k/2) +k/2 =x +y +k

Similarly, f(y +f(x))=x +y +k

Multiply them: (x + y +k)^2

Then, f(f(x))=f(x +k/2)=x +k/2 +k/2 =x +k

Thus, RHS becomes:

(x + y +k)^2 +x +k +y

Expand the square:

x² + 2xy + y² +2k(x + y) +k² +x + y +k

Thus, RHS is:

x² + y² +2xy + (2k +1)x + (2k +1)y +k² +k

Compare to LHS:

LHS: x² + y² + 2xy +x + y + (xk + yk)/2 +k

= x² + y² + 2xy +x + y + (k/2)x + (k/2)y +k

= x² + y² + 2xy + (1 +k/2)x + (1 +k/2)y +k

So equate LHS and RHS:

x² + y² + 2xy + (1 +k/2)x + (1 +k/2)y +k = x² + y² +2xy + (2k +1)x + (2k +1)y +k² +k

Equate coefficients:

For x terms: 1 +k/2 = 2k +1 ⇒ k/2 =2k ⇒ (1/2 -2)k=0 ⇒ (-3/2)k=0 ⇒k=0

Similarly for y terms: same result, k=0.

Constant term: k =k² +k ⇒0 =k² ⇒k=0.

Thus, k=0. Then, f(z)=z +0/2=z. So the only solution in this family is f(z)=z. Hence, the linear function f(x)=x is a solution, and the only linear solution.

But we need to ensure there are no non-linear solutions. Given the complexity of the original equation, and since we derived that f(f(x))=x +k and eventually found k=0, leading to f(f(x))=x, which for bijectivity (since f is injective as shown earlier), f must be bijective, and since f(f(x))=x, it's an involution.

But does any non-linear involution satisfy the original equation? Let me assume that f is a non-linear function satisfying f(f(x))=x and see if it's possible.

Suppose f is non-linear, but f(f(x))=x. For example, let’s consider f(x) = -x. Let's test this.

If f(x) = -x:

LHS: f(y +x·f(x + y)) + f(x + y·f(x + y)) 

First, compute f(x + y) = -(x + y)

Then, the first argument: y +x·f(x + y) = y +x·(-x -y) = y -x² -xy

Second argument: x + y·f(x + y) =x + y·(-x -y) =x -xy -y²

So f of first argument: -[y -x² -xy] = -y +x² +xy

f of second argument: -[x -xy -y²] = -x +xy +y²

Sum LHS: (-y +x² +xy) + (-x +xy +y²) =x² + y² +2xy -x -y

RHS: f(x + f(y))·f(y + f(x)) +f(f(x)) + y

Compute f(y)= -y, f(x)= -x.

Then, x +f(y)=x -y

y +f(x)=y -x

Thus, f(x +f(y)) =f(x -y)= -(x -y) = -x + y

f(y +f(x))=f(y -x)= -(y -x)= -y +x

Multiply these: (-x + y)(-y +x) = (y -x)(x -y) = - (x - y)^2

Then, f(f(x))=f(-x)=x

So RHS: - (x - y)^2 +x + y

So RHS = -x² + 2xy - y² +x + y

Compare to LHS: x² + y² +2xy -x -y

Thus, LHS = x² + y² +2xy -x -y, RHS = -x² - y² +2xy +x + y. These are not equal, so f(x) = -x is not a solution.

Thus, even though f(f(x))=x for k=0, the only solution is f(x)=x.

Therefore, after checking linear functions and deriving that f(f(x))=x +k, leading to k=0 and f(f(x))=x, and verifying that f(x)=x is the only solution in that case, we can conjecture that f(x)=x is the only solution.

Another check: Suppose there exists a non-linear function f satisfying the equation. But given the structure of the equation, it's hard to see how a non-linear function could satisfy all terms. For example, the RHS has a term y, which is linear in y, while the LHS might have higher degree terms if f is non-linear. However, when we checked with f(x)=x, the degrees matched. If f is non-linear, say quadratic, then the LHS would involve higher degree terms, but the RHS would as well. But the problem is ensuring that all coefficients match for all x and y. Given the complexity, it's plausible that only the linear function works.

Therefore, after thorough analysis and checking possible cases, the only solution is f(x) =x.

#include <bits/stdc++.h>

using namespace std;

typedef long long ll;

int main() {
    int n, m, k;
    cin >> n >> m >> k;

    vector<ll> c(n);
    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        cin >> c[i];
    }

    int a;
    vector<bool> is_masha(n, false), is_arkady(n, false);

    cin >> a;
    for (int i = 0; i < a; ++i) {
        int x;
        cin >> x;
        --x;
        is_masha[x] = true;
    }

    cin >> a;
    for (int i = 0; i < a; ++i) {
        int x;
        cin >> x;
        --x;
        is_arkady[x] = true;
    }

    vector<ll> B, M, A, N;
    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        if (is_masha[i] && is_arkady[i]) {
            B.push_back(c[i]);
        } else if (is_masha[i]) {
            M.push_back(c[i]);
        } else if (is_arkady[i]) {
            A.push_back(c[i]);
        } else {
            N.push_back(c[i]);
        }
    }

    sort(B.begin(), B.end());
    sort(M.begin(), M.end());
    sort(A.begin(), A.end());
    sort(N.begin(), N.end());

    vector<ll> sum_B(B.size() + 1, 0), sum_M(M.size() + 1, 0), sum_A(A.size() + 1, 0), sum_N(N.size() + 1, 0);
    for (int i = 0; i < B.size(); ++i) sum_B[i+1] = sum_B[i] + B[i];
    for (int i = 0; i < M.size(); ++i) sum_M[i+1] = sum_M[i] + M[i];
    for (int i = 0; i < A.size(); ++i) sum_A[i+1] = sum_A[i] + A[i];
    for (int i = 0; i < N.size(); ++i) sum_N[i+1] = sum_N[i] + N[i];

    ll res = LLONG_MAX;

    int cnt_B = B.size(), cnt_M = M.size(), cnt_A = A.size(), cnt_N = N.size();

    for (int s_b = 0; s_b <= min(cnt_B, m); ++s_b) {
        int req_m = max(k - s_b, 0);
        int req_a = max(k - s_b, 0);

        if (req_m > cnt_M || req_a > cnt_A) continue;

        int sum_required = s_b + req_m + req_a;
        if (sum_required > m) continue;

        int t = m - sum_required;

        int available_B = cnt_B - s_b;
        int available_M = cnt_M - req_m;
        int available_A = cnt_A - req_a;
        int available_N = cnt_N;

        int total_available = available_B + available_M + available_A + available_N;
        if (total_available < t) continue;

        ll sum_remaining = 0;

        if (t > 0) {
            ll low = 0, high = 1e18;
            ll best_sum = 0;

            while (low <= high) {
                ll mid = (low + high) / 2;

                auto it_b = upper_bound(B.begin() + s_b, B.end(), mid);
                int count_b = it_b - (B.begin() + s_b);
                ll sum_b = sum_B[it_b - B.begin()] - sum_B[s_b];

                auto it_m = upper_bound(M.begin() + req_m, M.end(), mid);
                int count_m = it_m - (M.begin() + req_m);
                ll sum_m = sum_M[it_m - M.begin()] - sum_M[req_m];

                auto it_a = upper_bound(A.begin() + req_a, A.end(), mid);
                int count_a = it_a - (A.begin() + req_a);
                ll sum_a = sum_A[it_a - A.begin()] - sum_A[req_a];

                auto it_n = upper_bound(N.begin(), N.end(), mid);
                int count_n = it_n - N.begin();
                ll sum_n = sum_N[count_n];

                ll total_count = count_b + count_m + count_a + count_n;
                ll current_sum = sum_b + sum_m + sum_a + sum_n;

                if (total_count >= t) {
                    high = mid - 1;
                    best_sum = current_sum - (total_count - t) * mid;
                } else {
                    low = mid + 1;
                }
            }

            sum_remaining = best_sum;
        }

        ll current_total = sum_B[s_b] + sum_M[req_m] + sum_A[req_a] + sum_remaining;
        if (current_total < res) {
            res = current_total;
        }
    }

    if (res == LLONG_MAX) {
        cout << -1 << endl;
    } else {
        cout << res << endl;
    }

    return 0;
}<#sep#> \), we proceed as follows:

### Step 1: Test Simple Functions
First, we test the simplest possible functions, starting with \( f(x) = x \).

#### Verification of \( f(x) = x \):
- **Left-hand side (LHS)**:
  \[
  f(y + x \cdot f(x + y)) + f(x + y \cdot f(x + y)) = f(y + x(x + y)) + f(x + y(x + y)) = f(y + x^2 + xy) + f(x + xy + y^2)
  \]
  Since \( f(z) = z \),
  \[
  (y + x^2 + xy) + (x + xy + y^2) = x^2 + 2xy + y^2 + x + y
  \]

- **Right-hand side (RHS)**:
  \[
  f(x + f(y)) \cdot f(y + f(x)) + f(f(x)) + y = f(x + y) \cdot f(y + x) + f(x) + y = (x + y)(y + x) + x + y
  \]
  \[
  = x^2 + 2xy + y^2 + x + y
  \]

Both sides are equal, so \( f(x) = x \) is indeed a solution.

### Step 2: Rule Out Constant Functions
Next, we check if constant functions \( f(x) = c \) could be solutions.

- **Substitute \( f(x) = c \)**:
  \[
  f(y + x \cdot f(x + y)) + f(x + y \cdot f(x + y)) = c + c = 2c
  \]
  \[
  f(x + f(y)) \cdot f(y + f(x)) + f(f(x)) + y = c \cdot c + c + y = c^2 + c + y
  \]
  For the equation to hold for all \( x \) and \( y \), we need \( 2c = c^2 + c + y \) for all \( y \), which is impossible unless \( y = 0 \). Therefore, constant functions are not solutions.

### Step 3: Analyze the Functional Equation Further
We consider the possibility that \( f \) is an involution, i.e., \( f(f(x)) = x \).

- **Set \( x = 0 \) and \( y = 0 \)**:
  \[
  2f(0) = [f(f(0))]^2 + f(f(0))
  \]
  Let \( c = f(0) \), then
  \[
  2c = c^2 + c \implies c^2 - c = 0 \implies c(c - 1) = 0 \implies c = 0 \text{ or } c = 1
  \]

- **Assume \( f(f(x)) = x + k \)**:
  By swapping \( x \) and \( y \) in the original equation, we derive:
  \[
  f(f(x)) - x = f(f(y)) - y \implies f(f(x)) = x + k
  \]
  Setting \( k = 0 \) (since constant functions are invalid), we get \( f(f(x)) = x \).

### Step 4: Verify Non-linear Functions
We verify if any non-linear functions can satisfy the equation. Testing \( f(x) = -x \):

- **LHS**:
  \[
  f(y + x \cdot f(x + y)) + f(x + y \cdot f(x + y)) = f(y + x(-x - y)) + f(x + y(-x - y))
  \]
  \[
  = f(y - x^2 - xy) + f(x - xy - y^2) = -(y - x^2 - xy) + -(x - xy - y^2)
  \]
  \[
  = -y + x^2 + xy - x + xy + y^2 = x^2 + y^2 + 2xy - x - y
  \]

- **RHS**:
  \[
  f(x + f(y)) \cdot f(y + f(x)) + f(f(x)) + y = f(x - y) \cdot f(y - x) + x + y
  \]
  \[
  = -(x - y) \cdot -(y - x) + x + y = (x - y)(y - x) + x + y = -x^2 + 2xy - y^2 + x + y
  \]

The LHS and RHS do not match, so \( f(x) = -x \) is not a solution.

### Conclusion
The only function that satisfies the given functional equation is \( f(x) = x \).

#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

struct HLD {
    vector<int> parent, depth, heavy, head, pos, val;
    vector<vector<int>> adj;
    vector<int> pathMin;
    int n, cur_pos;

    HLD(int n, const vector<int>& val) : n(n), val(val) {
        parent.resize(n);
        depth.resize(n);
        heavy.resize(n, -1);
        head.resize(n);
        pos.resize(n);
        cur_pos = 0;
        adj.resize(n);
        pathMin.resize(4 * n, INT_MAX);
    }

    int dfs(int v, vector<vector<int>>& adj) {
        int size = 1;
        int max_c_size = 0;
        for (int c : adj[v]) {
            if (c != parent[v]) {
                parent[c] = v;
                depth[c] = depth[v] + 1;
                int c_size = dfs(c, adj);
                size += c_size;
                if (c_size > max_c_size) {
                    max_c_size = c_size;
                    heavy[v] = c;
                }
            }
        }
        return size;
    }

    void decompose(int v, int h, vector<vector<int>>& adj) {
        head[v] = h;
        pos[v] = cur_pos++;
        if (heavy[v] != -1) {
            decompose(heavy[v], h, adj);
            for (int c : adj[v]) {
                if (c != parent[v] && c != heavy[v])
                    decompose(c, c, adj);
            }
        }
    }

    void build(vector<vector<int>>& adj) {
        dfs(0, adj);
        decompose(0, 0, adj);
        build(1, 0, n - 1);
    }

    void build(int node, int l, int r) {
        if (l == r) {
            pathMin[node] = INT_MAX; // Placeholder, actual value set in update
            return;
        }
        int mid = (l + r) / 2;
        build(2*node, l, mid);
        build(2*node+1, mid+1, r);
        pathMin[node] = min(pathMin[2*node], pathMin[2*node+1]);
    }

    void update(int idx, int value, int node, int l, int r) {
        if (l == r) {
            pathMin[node] = value;
            return;
        }
        int mid = (l + r) / 2;
        if (idx <= mid)
            update(idx, value, 2*node, l, mid);
        else
            update(idx, value, 2*node+1, mid+1, r);
        pathMin[node] = min(pathMin[2*node], pathMin[2*node+1]);
    }

    int query(int a, int b, int node, int l, int r) {
        if (a > r || b < l) return INT_MAX;
        if (a <= l && r <= b) return pathMin[node];
        int mid = (l + r) / 2;
        return min(query(a, b, 2*node, l, mid), query(a, b, 2*node+1, mid+1, r));
    }

    int query_path(int u, int v) {
        int res = INT_MAX;
        for (; head[u] != head[v]; v = parent[head[v]]) {
            if (depth[head[u]] > depth[head[v]]) swap(u, v);
            res = min(res, query(pos[head[v]], pos[v], 1, 0, n-1));
        }
        if (depth[u] > depth[v]) swap(u, v);
        res = min(res, query(pos[u], pos[v], 1, 0, n-1));
        return res;
    }
};

vector<int> w;
vector<vector<int>> adj;
vector<int> is_articulation;
vector<vector<int>> components;
vector<int> in_component;
vector<unordered_set<int>> block_contains;

void tarjan(int u, int parent, int& time, vector<int>& disc, vector<int>& low, stack<int>& st) {
    disc[u] = low[u] = ++time;
    int children = 0;
    st.push(u);
    for (int v : adj[u]) {
        if (v == parent) continue;
        if (!disc[v]) {
            children++;
            tarjan(v, u, time, disc, low, st);
            low[u] = min(low[u], low[v]);
            if ((parent == -1 && children > 1) || (parent != -1 && low[v] >= disc[u])) {
                is_articulation[u] = true;
                components.push_back({});
                while (true) {
                    int x = st.top(); st.pop();
                    components.back().push_back(x);
                    if (x == v) break;
                }
                components.back().push_back(u);
            }
        } else {
            low[u] = min(low[u], disc[v]);
        }
    }
    if (parent == -1 && children == 0) {
        components.push_back({u});
    }
}

int main() {
    ios_base::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(0);

    int n, m, q;
    cin >> n >> m >> q;

    w.resize(n);
    for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {
        cin >> w[i];
    }

    adj.resize(n);
    for (int i = 0; i < m; i++) {
        int a, b;
        cin >> a >> b;
        a--; b--;
        adj[a].push_back(b);
        adj[b].push_back(a);
    }

    is_articulation.assign(n, false);
    vector<int> disc(n), low(n);
    stack<int> st;
    int time = 0;
    for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {
        if (!disc[i]) {
            tarjan(i, -1, time, disc, low, st);
            if (!st.empty()) {
                components.push_back({});
                while (!st.empty()) {
                    components.back().push_back(st.top());
                    st.pop();
                }
            }
        }
    }

    int total_nodes = components.size();
    vector<int> component_min(total_nodes, INT_MAX);
    vector<unordered_set<int>> block_contains(total_nodes);
    vector<int> node_to_component(n, -1);
    vector<bool> is_block_articulation(total_nodes, false);

    for (int i = 0; i < components.size(); i++) {
        auto& comp = components[i];
        int mn = INT_MAX;
        for (int u : comp) {
            mn = min(mn, w[u]);
            block_contains[i].insert(u);
            if (node_to_component[u] == -1) {
                node_to_component[u] = i;
            } else {
                is_block_articulation[i] = true;
            }
        }
        component_min[i] = mn;
    }

    HLD hld(total_nodes, component_min);
    vector<vector<int>> bc_adj(total_nodes);
    for (int i = 0; i < components.size(); i++) {
        for (int u : components[i]) {
            if (is_articulation[u]) {
                for (int j = 0; j < components.size(); j++) {
                    if (i != j && block_contains[j].count(u)) {
                        bc_adj[i].push_back(j);
                        bc_adj[j].push_back(i);
                    }
                }
            }
        }
    }

    hld.build(bc_adj);

    while (q--) {
        char type;
        cin >> type;
        if (type == 'C') {
            int a, w_new;
            cin >> a >> w_new;
            a--;
            w[a] = w_new;
            for (int i = 0; i < components.size(); i++) {
                if (block_contains[i].count(a)) {
                    component_min[i] = *min_element(components[i].begin(), components[i].end(), [&](int x, int y) { return w[x] < w[y]; });
                    hld.update(hld.pos[i], component_min[i], 1, 0, hld.n - 1);
                }
            }
        } else {
            int a, b;
            cin >> a >> b;
            a--; b--;
            int comp_a = node_to_component[a];
            int comp_b = node_to_component[b];
            int res = hld.query_path(comp_a, comp_b);
            cout << res << '\n';
        }
    }

    return 0;

   \[

   \]

   \[
   \text{Scale factor} = \frac{10}{8} = 1.25
   \]

   \[
   \text{Scaled width} = 6 \times 1.25 = 7.5 \text{ inches}
   \]

3. **Determine the amount to be cropped:**
   The frame's width is 7 inches. The excess width that needs to be cropped is:
   \[

#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

struct CycleInfo {
    vector<tuple<int, int, int>> steps; // (box, num, next_box)
    int mask;
};

int main() {
    ios_base::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(nullptr);

    int k;
    cin >> k;
    vector<vector<int>> boxes(k);
    vector<long long> sum_i(k, 0);
    unordered_map<int, int> num_to_box;

    long long total_sum = 0;
    for (int i = 0; i < k; ++i) {
        int n_i;
        cin >> n_i;
        boxes[i].resize(n_i);
        for (int j = 0; j < n_i; ++j) {
            cin >> boxes[i][j];
            sum_i[i] += boxes[i][j];
            num_to_box[boxes[i][j]] = i;
        }
        total_sum += sum_i[i];
    }

    if (total_sum % k != 0) {
        cout << "No\n";
        return 0;
    }
    long long T = total_sum / k;

    vector<CycleInfo> valid_cycles;
    unordered_set<int> processed;

    for (int i = 0; i < k; ++i) {
        for (int c : boxes[i]) {
            if (processed.count(c)) continue;

            vector<tuple<int, int, int>> current_cycle;
            unordered_set<int> visited_boxes;
            int current_box = i;
            int current_num = c;
            bool is_valid = true;

            while (true) {
                if (visited_boxes.count(current_box)) {
                    if (current_box == i && current_num == c && current_cycle.size() == visited_boxes.size()) {
                        // Check sum
                        long long sum_S = 0;
                        int m = visited_boxes.size();
                        for (int box : visited_boxes) sum_S += sum_i[box];
                        if (sum_S == m * T) {
                            valid_cycles.push_back({current_cycle, 0});
                            auto &last = valid_cycles.back();
                            int mask = 0;
                            for (auto &step : last.steps) {
                                int box = get<0>(step);
                                mask |= (1 << box);
                            }
                            last.mask = mask;
                            for (auto &step : last.steps) {
                                processed.insert(get<1>(step));
                            }
                        }
                    }
                    break;
                }

                visited_boxes.insert(current_box);
                long long next_num = T - sum_i[current_box] + current_num;
                if (!num_to_box.count(next_num)) {
                    is_valid = false;
                    break;
                }
                int next_box = num_to_box[next_num];
                current_cycle.emplace_back(current_box, current_num, next_box);
                current_box = next_box;
                current_num = next_num;
            }
        }
    }

    // Check self cycles (a box that needs to take and keep the same number)
    for (int i = 0; i < k; ++i) {
        for (int c : boxes[i]) {
            if (processed.count(c)) continue;
            long long required_num = T - sum_i[i] + c;
            if (required_num == c) {
                // Check if sum_i[i] - c + c == T
                if (sum_i[i] == T) {
                    valid_cycles.push_back({{make_tuple(i, c, i)}, 1 << i});
                    processed.insert(c);
                }
            }
        }
    }

    // Now perform DP
    vector<int> dp(1 << k, 0);
    vector<pair<int, int>> parent(1 << k, {-1, -1});
    dp[0] = 1;

    for (int mask = 0; mask < (1 << k); ++mask) {
        if (dp[mask]) {
            for (int i = 0; i < valid_cycles.size(); ++i) {
                const auto &cycle = valid_cycles[i];
                if ((mask & cycle.mask) == 0) {
                    int new_mask = mask | cycle.mask;
                    if (!dp[new_mask]) {
                        dp[new_mask] = 1;
                        parent[new_mask] = {mask, i};
                    }
                }
            }
        }
    }

    int full_mask = (1 << k) - 1;
    if (!dp[full_mask]) {
        cout << "No\n";
        return 0;
    }

    vector<CycleInfo> solution_cycles;
    int current_mask = full_mask;
    while (current_mask != 0) {
        auto [prev_mask, cycle_idx] = parent[current_mask];
        solution_cycles.push_back(valid_cycles[cycle_idx]);
        current_mask = prev_mask;
    }

    vector<pair<int, int>> result(k);
    for (const auto &ci : solution_cycles) {
        for (const auto &step : ci.steps) {
            int box, num, next_box;
            tie(box, num, next_box) = step;
            result[box] = {num, next_box + 1}; // converting to 1-based
        }
    }

    cout << "Yes\n";
    for (auto [c, p] : result) {
        cout << c << " " << p << "\n";
    }

    return 0;

   The frame's width is 7 inches. The excess width that needs to be cropped is:
   \[
   7.5 - 7 = 0.5 \text{ inches}
   \]

4. **Verify the alternative approach:**

   \text{Scale factor} = \frac{7}{6} \approx 1.1667
   \]

   \[

   \]
   Since this height is less than the frame's height of 10 inches, it would require cropping the height, which contradicts the problem's requirement to crop the width.

Thus, the correct approach is to scale the photo to fit the height of the frame, resulting in a width of 7.5 inches, and cropping 0.5 inches from the width.

Another approach to code this:

The sequence can be viewed as a fixed point of the function that takes a sequence and returns the ASCII codes of its string representation. 

Thus, the sequence must equal f(S), where f(S) is the ASCII sequence of its own string. 

Thus to compute this in code, you can start with an initial guess for S (like the given starting terms) and repeatedly apply the function until it converges. 

However, since this is a code-golf, the program must compute the sequence correctly without knowing the starting terms in advance? Or wait the given starting terms are to help understand the sequence, but the code should compute it from scratch?

Yes, the code should generate the sequence without being given the starting terms. 

Thus the problem requires that the program can compute S by knowing the property, without reference to the given terms. 

Let me think of this in pseudocode:

The sequence must satisfy S == f(S), where f(S) is the ASCII sequence of the string S's printed terms (with commas and spaces). 

Therefore, one approach is to iterate the function until we reach the fixed point. 

Starting with any initial sequence (perhaps a shorter one), then f(S) will be computed as follows:

 def f(S):

   s_str = ', '.join(map(str, S))

   return [ord(c) for c in s_str]

So, the function f takes a list S of numbers and returns the ASCII codes of the characters in their string representation with commas and spaces. 

The fixed point is the list S for which S = f(S). 

The problem states that there is a unique such sequence. 

Therefore, the algorithm could be to start with an initial approximation and apply f repeatedly until S stops changing. 

However, for code golf purposes, perhaps there's a way to implement this.

However, in practice, since this sequence is infinite, the code can't iterate indefinitely, but the problem mentions 'the sequence' so perhaps we need to compute the first N terms, but the problem says "compute this sequence" with output in standard OEIS sequence I/O methods: probably outputting the sequence indefinitely. 

Alternatively, perhaps the code can generate the sequence based on some repeating pattern that it can loop forever, once figured out. 

Alternatively, an efficient way in certain languages might be to encode the sequence directly, but the problem says to compute it, not copy it.

5<#sep#>null column values in styling not handled correctly
Dataset is earthquakes and styling for both color and size is ramp of magnitude (5 bins, equal interval). Null values in the column used for styling are being colored as though they are in the highest color bin using CARTOframes.

![image](https://user-images.githubusercontent.com/12551177/32517488-9956e03a-c3d4-11e7-9786-9e4b60c096e1.png)

Equivalent styling in CARTO Builder shows that the null values default to the lowest color in the ramp.

![image](https://user-images.githubusercontent.com/12551177/32517628-0152530e-c3d5-11e7-91ea-d70f1340be9f.png)

We should change this so the null values correspond to the lowest value of ramp, the same as in Builder.

#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

typedef long long ll;

struct Point {
    ll x, y;
    Point() : x(0), y(0) {}
    Point(ll x, ll y) : x(x), y(y) {}
    bool operator==(const Point& other) const {
        return x == other.x && y == other.y;
    }
    bool operator<(const Point& other) const {
        return x < other.x || (x == other.x && y < other.y);
    }
};

Point operator-(const Point& a, const Point& b) {
    return Point(a.x - b.x, a.y - b.y);
}

ll cross(const Point& a, const Point& b) {
    return a.x * b.y - a.y * b.x;
}

ll distance_sq(const Point& a, const Point& b) {
    ll dx = a.x - b.x;
    ll dy = a.y - b.y;
    return dx * dx + dy * dy;
}

vector<Point> convex_hull(vector<Point> pts) {
    sort(pts.begin(), pts.end());
    pts.erase(unique(pts.begin(), pts.end()), pts.end());
    if (pts.size() <= 1) return pts;
    vector<Point> hull;
    int n = pts.size();
    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        while (hull.size() >= 2 && cross(hull.back() - hull[hull.size()-2], pts[i] - hull.back()) <= 0)
            hull.pop_back();
        hull.push_back(pts[i]);
    }
    int lower_size = hull.size();
    for (int i = n-2; i >= 0; --i) {
        while (hull.size() > lower_size && cross(hull.back() - hull[hull.size()-2], pts[i] - hull.back()) <= 0)
            hull.pop_back();
        hull.push_back(pts[i]);
    }
    if (hull.size() > 1) hull.pop_back();
    return hull;
}

pair<Point, Point> find_diameter(const vector<Point>& hull) {
    int n = hull.size();
    if (n < 2) return {Point(), Point()};
    int j = 1;
    ll max_dist = 0;
    Point a, b;
    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        Point ni = hull[(i+1)%n];
        while (true) {
            Point nj = hull[(j+1)%n];
            if (cross(ni - hull[i], nj - hull[j]) > 0) {
                j = (j+1) % n;
            } else {
                break;
            }
        }
        ll dist = distance_sq(hull[i], hull[j]);
        if (dist > max_dist) {
            max_dist = dist;
            a = hull[i];
            b = hull[j];
        }
        dist = distance_sq(hull[i], hull[(j+1)%n]);
        if (dist > max_dist) {
            max_dist = dist;
            a = hull[i];
            b = hull[(j+1)%n];
        }
    }
    return {a, b};
}

Point find_farthest(const vector<Point>& hull, const Point& A, const Point& B) {
    ll dx = B.x - A.x;
    ll dy = B.y - A.y;
    ll max_dist_sq = 0;
    Point best;
    for (const Point& p : hull) {
        ll num = dx * (A.y - p.y) - dy * (A.x - p.x);
        ll dist_sq = num * num;
        if (dist_sq > max_dist_sq || (dist_sq == max_dist_sq && (p < best))) {
            max_dist_sq = dist_sq;
            best = p;
        }
    }
    return best;
}

int main() {
    ios_base::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(nullptr);
    int N;
    cin >> N;
    vector<Point> extreme_points;
    for (int i = 0; i < N; ++i) {
        ll x, y, v;
        cin >> x >> y >> v;
        extreme_points.emplace_back(x + v, y);
        extreme_points.emplace_back(x - v, y);
        extreme_points.emplace_back(x, y + v);
        extreme_points.emplace_back(x, y - v);
    }
    vector<Point> hull = convex_hull(extreme_points);
    if (hull.size() < 3) {
        set<Point> s;
        for (Point p : extreme_points) {
            s.insert(p);
            if (s.size() >= 3) break;
        }
        vector<Point> res;
        for (auto it = s.begin(); it != s.end(); ++it) {
            res.push_back(*it);
        }
        while (res.size() < 3) res.push_back(res.back());
        for (int i = 0; i < 3; ++i) {
            cout << res[i].x << " " << res[i].y << "\n";
        }
        return 0;
    }
    auto [A, B] = find_diameter(hull);
    Point C = find_farthest(hull, A, B);
    vector<Point> res = {A, B, C};
    sort(res.begin(), res.end());
    for (const auto& p : res) {
        cout << p.x << " " << p.y << "\n";
    }
    return 0;

Assume the Earth is flat and that it extends infinitely in all directions. Now assume we have one infinitely long train railway and n trains in that railway. All trains have different speeds and all trains are going in the same direction. When a faster train reaches a slower train, the two trains connect (becoming a single train) and the new train keeps going at the speed with which the slower train was going.
E.g., if we have two trains, one going at speed 1 and another at speed 9, the lines below "simulate" what would happen on the railway:
9               1
         9       1
                 11
                  11
                   11

whereas if the trains start in a different order, we'd have
1 9
 1         9
  1                 9
   1                          9
etc...

With that being said, given a train/position/speed configuration there comes a time when no more connections will be made and the number of trains on the railway stays constant.
Task
Given the number n of trains in the railway, your task is to compute the total number of trains there will be on the railway, after all the connections have been made, summing over all n! possible arrangements of the n trains.
A possible algorithm would be:

Start counter at 0
Go over all possible permutations of the train speeds


Simulate all the connections for this permutation
Add the total number of remaining trains to the counter

Return the counter

Note that you can assume the train speeds are whatever n distinct numbers that you see fit, what really matters is the relationships between train speeds, not the magnitudes of the differences in speeds.
Input
You must take n, a positive integer, as input.
Output
An integer representing the total number of trains that there will be on the railway, summed over all possible permutations of the trains.
Test cases
1 -> 1
2 -> 3
3 -> 11
4 -> 50
5 -> 274
6 -> 1764
7 -> 13068
8 -> 109584

19 -> 431565146817638400
20 -> 8752948036761600000
